Nothing here is verified against the official text, and no one should read it as legal advice.

## Seat

The seat is a party to a civil action in the Singapore courts (a claimant, a defendant, a third party or an appellant), asking, on facts they can state, what they must file or serve, by when, how, at what cost, what the Court may order, and what follows if they do not.
The seat layer is `roc10-seat.l4`, with cases in `roc10-cases-seat.l4`; every question begins "Under Order N, rule M," (the Rules' own numbering).
There is no seat for the Court, the Registrar, the Commissioner for Labour, the Comptroller, the Attorney-General or the Public Trustee: a provision that addresses only one of them is encoded in layer 1 for what it decides, and in the seat only for what it means to the party (what they will be directed to do, what they may do, what they must be given).
Orders 54 and 55 are the clearest case.
Order 54 is mostly about the officer who administers an oath or affirmation, and its seat is the person taking the oath or making the affirmation; Order 55 is mostly about the Registrar, the Attorney-General and the examiner, and its seat is the witness or applicant in a proceeding to obtain evidence for a court outside Singapore.
A question such as "has the Registrar sent the document to the Attorney-General as the Registrar must" or "may the Registrar exercise the power to make the order" is therefore asked by that party, about what they can expect or require of the officer; it is never a question the officer asks about their own duty (some of those duties, written as duties, are in `roc10-duties.l4`).

## Where this file departs from PLAN.md

`PLAN.md` section 2 reads a stated period as a limit "in its own wording" and answers `NOTHING` outside it.
The brief for this part (the ruling of 2026-11-05 on periods, "where" and scope limits, quoted in the Fork register below) governs where the two differ, and says: outside a period the text fixes in its own words, `JUST FALSE`; a "where ..." (and "when", "upon", "whenever") condition that is not met, `JUST FALSE`; a scope limit with no "only", `NOTHING`.
This part follows the brief.
Every branch that rests on it is listed under "Marginal readings for a lawyer", with the `NOTHING` reading that `PLAN.md` would give as the other reading.

## Fork register (roc10)

### The four rulings, word for word as printed in the brief

These are Meng's rulings of 2026-11-04 and 2026-11-05.
They bind this part; L4 itself takes no position on them.
In this part: E1 option C applies everywhere (each section answers on its own), and no function gathers a legal effect by name; N2: a flat negative ("shall not", "is not", "no person", "may not", "must not") is answered `JUST FALSE`, with the Hohfeld kinds recorded under `## Jural relations (roc10)`; the periods ruling and the delegated-definitions ruling are applied everywhere in the range (each site is listed under "Marginal readings for a lawyer (roc10)" or "Judgment calls").

- **E1** (bench `commons-negation-1030`; should a legal effect be one definition gathered from every section that gives it?): option B, as guidance for gathering a legal effect's routes by hand only, with no pilot run; option A (gathering by bare name) is withdrawn as a direction for the language, not deferred; option C (per-section answers) applies everywhere else; the Magna Carta pilot in the printed recommendation (chapters 2, 3, 4, 5, 37, 43, 46 and 53, with a blind second reader) is not to be run. Meng's lean, verbatim: "B but generally uncomfortable with this much magic because it takes the language from unopinionated to opinionated in ways that are hard to teach an l4 novice." Meng's note, verbatim: "Fundamentally I feel like we are trying to make the language do work that the human l4 drafter should do. Too much magic. Can’t we just decline to be this opinionated and say the programmer needs to do this thinking?" Marked 2026-11-03T23:19:02.812Z, ruled 2026-11-04. Bench: https://claude.ai/artifact/49q6k2H5xtuJqy5ttkwZ23
- **N2** (bench `commons-negation-1030`; does a flat negative count as the text's no?): option A as printed: flat negatives count as a no, and where the text names the bearer the prohibition (`SHANT`) is derived from that answer; the Hohfeld kinds stay in each row's "Jural relations" table; option C's wording (the kind typed in the person-facing answer) comes only after a lawyer confirms the typing, decided together with the S/E choice for how "not covered" is said. Meng's lean, verbatim: "Why are we even trying to do this work? Some of these rules are metatextual and require more homoiconicity than we currently have in L4." Meng's note: none written. Marked 2026-11-03T23:22:49.971Z, ruled 2026-11-04. Bench: https://claude.ai/artifact/49q6k2H5xtuJqy5ttkwZ23
- **Periods, "where" and scope limits** (a question raised on 2026-11-05 while checking an encoding of the Retirement and Re-employment Act 1993, not a bench): (a) a period the text fixes in its own words ("within one month", "begins from ... until the employee attains the age", "on the last day of employment"): outside the period the answer is `JUST FALSE`; (b) a "where ..." condition: when it fails the answer is `JUST FALSE`; (c) a scope limit with no "only" (for example "below" the minimum age): outside the scope the answer is `NOTHING`. Meng's words, verbatim apart from the trigger word that ended the message: "i agree with your lean: no for a and b; nothing for c." Typed 2026-11-05T04:30:00.322Z, ruled 2026-11-05.
- **Delegated definitions, defences and "until" bars** (questions raised on 2026-11-05 while checking encodings of the Proceeds of Crime Act 2002, the Terrorism Act 2000 and the Singapore Sale of Goods Act 1979, not a bench): (a) a definition the text itself hands to a list ("Schedule 9 has effect for determining what is a business in the regulated sector"): when no head in the list holds, the answer is `JUST FALSE`; a bare list with no such hand-off still answers `NOTHING`; (b) "it is a defence ... to prove that X": when X is not proved, the answer is `JUST FALSE` for that provision's defence, worded "this section gives no defence on these facts" and never "no defence at all"; (c) "when", "upon" and "whenever" count as "where" under rule (b) of the periods ruling above; (d) "the property does not pass until X": encode the bar ("the property cannot yet pass": `JUST TRUE` before X, `JUST FALSE` after X) and never claim that the property has passed. Meng approved these four readings as put to him by the general-manager session, in the word NOTHINGBURGER; his note on (d), verbatim apart from the bracket and the trigger word that ended the message: "in the case of Q5 [the "until" bar] i hope we are using the deontic mechanisms to examine a trace." The note is a request about how (d) is modelled, not part of the ruling. Typed 2026-11-05T06:04:54.553Z, ruled 2026-11-05.

### Sweep of "upon", "when" and "whenever" (fix round, 2026-11-05)

Ruling (c) of the delegated-definitions ruling above says "when", "upon" and "whenever" count as "where", and an unmet "where" answers `JUST FALSE`.
Every occurrence in lines 6102 to 6795 was listed with `awk 'NR>=6102 && NR<=6795' inputs/SCJA1969-S914-2021.txt | grep -i -E '\b(upon|whenever|when)\b'`: "whenever" occurs nowhere; "upon" on 6 lines; "when" on 16 lines (17 matches).
What each answers now (text line; the function; the word; changed or not):

| text line | function | the words | answer when unmet | changed in the fix round |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6156 | `o 46A r 3(4) the Court may allow the page limit to be exceeded`, roc10-order-46a.l4:430 | "upon the payment of the fees prescribed" (unless waived under paragraph (5)) | `JUST FALSE` | yes, from `NOTHING` |
| 6157 | `o 46A r 3(5) the Court may waive, refund, defer or apportion the payment of the fees`, roc10-order-46a.l4:443 | "upon application" | `JUST FALSE` | yes, from `NOTHING` |
| 6137, 6139 | `o 46A r 1(7)(a)`, `(b)` and `(c)`, roc10-order-46a.l4:191 | "when a Court makes an order" (placing, removing) | `JUST FALSE`; the order's date is a `MAYBE DATE` and layer 1 reads its `NOTHING` as "no such order was made" (fork 46A.5) | yes, from `NOTHING` |
| 6204 | `o 47 r 4(2) the duty to seal the document arises`, roc10-order-47.l4:307 | "upon the request of the applicant or the Comptroller" | `JUST FALSE` (tested; fork 47.2) | no |
| 6421 | `o 49 r 6(4) the Court may make such order and give such directions as it thinks fit`, roc10-order-49.l4:712 | "Upon the hearing" | `JUST FALSE` | no |
| 6534 | `o 53 r 2(2) the Court may discharge the order or vary it as it thinks fit`, roc10-order-53.l4:124 | "upon an application by the person required to comply" | `JUST FALSE` | no |
| 6731 | `o 56 r 2(2) the proceedings must continue in the name of the firm`, roc10-order-56.l4:99, and the function after it | "When the names of the partners have been declared" | `JUST FALSE` | no |
| 6762, 6768 | `o 56 r 5(3) an enforcement order against the member is barred`, roc10-order-56.l4:521 | "a member of the firm who was out of Singapore when the originating claim was issued": the "when" sits inside a description of the person | `NOTHING` (fork 49.1) | no |
| 6691 | `o 54 r 1(1) this Order applies`, roc10-order-54.l4:38 | "applies in every instance when an oath or affirmation is taken or made, and administered": a statement of scope with no "only" | `NOTHING` (periods ruling (c)); the other reading is `JUST FALSE` for an occasion that is not an oath or affirmation | no |

The other occurrences are nouns or the content of a list item, not conditions, and need no function: "the date when" (lines 6350, 6373, 6456, 6460), "the date upon which" (6466), "how and when the tenancy was determined" (6513), "indicate when it is intended" (6579, 6661), "describes when, where and how" (6682) and "at the time when the cause of action accrued" (6725, 6727).

## Jural relations (roc10)

Hohfeld kinds are recorded here and are not typed into any person-facing answer (ruling N2).
The holder, bearer and kind of each row were typed by this part from the text's own modal words ("must", "may", "must not", "no person may") and are assumed, not ruled; a lawyer should confirm the typing before any of it is used.
A provision that creates no right, privilege, power or immunity (a definition, a counting rule, a form, a rule of practice) is listed as `none`.
"Holder" is the party with the right, privilege, power or immunity; "bearer" is the party with the correlative duty, no-right, liability or disability; "the text names none" means the provision does not say.
The last column lists the L4 functions that carry the row (from the `-- COVERS` tags; the first three are shown).

## Marginal readings for a lawyer (roc10)

The brief names two readings for a lawyer, and this part took both as the status quo: (a) a failed "if" (and, by the ruling of 2026-11-05, a failed "where", "when", "upon", "whenever", "on", "after" or a missed stated period) counts as the text's no, clause by clause, within the provision that holds it, which is an inference ("if A then B" does not itself say "if not A then not B"); flat negatives count as the text saying no (ruling N2, so not listed again here); (b) a prohibition or grant limited by its own wording does not count as the text's no, and answers `NOTHING`.
The tables below are generated from the `-- FALSE-kind` and `-- NOTHING-kind` comments that sit on each branch in the `.l4` files, so every branch is cited by function, file and line.
Table A lists every branch that answers `JUST FALSE` because a condition the text sets is not met or a period it fixes was missed (188 branches); 38 further `JUST FALSE` branches rest on a flat negative, a plain "does not apply", an override ("despite") or a statement that a fact makes no difference ("whether ... or not", "nothing in this Rule affects") and are not listed (ruling N2: the text says no in its own words).
Table B lists every branch that answers `NOTHING` because the text limits a prohibition, grant or scope by its own wording (209 branches).
Table C lists the 19 branches that answer `JUST FALSE` because a definition says what a term "means" and the case is not that thing (the brief reads a definition that closes its own class as the text saying no).
The other `NOTHING` branches are not marginal readings: 6 are "includes", 6 are "list not closed", 9 are "points outside the text", 211 are "silent"; `silent` covers a bare "must" not done, a lifted bar that grants nothing, and a case the text does not address.
The other reading is the same for every row in a table and is stated in its heading; the text of the branch is the quotation or note the code carries.

### Table A: a failed condition or a missed period answers `JUST FALSE` (reading (a))

The other reading: `NOTHING`, because the text says what must be done on the facts it describes and is silent about every other case (an "if A then B" does not say "if not A then not B"; a period fixed in the text's words does not say that an act outside it is forbidden).

The other reading: `NOTHING`, because a definition says what a word means in this Order and says nothing about whether the case is a thing the Order governs.

### Reading a result: FALSE and NOTHING (roc10)

In this part a **no** (`JUST FALSE`) is the text saying no in its own words:
- a flat negative ("no person may inspect", "must not grant", "may not be made more than", "is not required"): ruling N2;
- a definition that "means" a closed thing, where the case is not that thing;
- a condition the text introduces with "if", "unless", "only if", or "where", "when", "upon", "whenever" (the ruling of 2026-11-05), when it is not met;
- a period the text fixes in its own words ("within 14 days after", "no later than", "at least 7 clear days before", "not later than"), when the act was done outside it (the same ruling, limb (a)).

**Not covered** (`NOTHING`) is anything else:
- a list the text does not close, or a definition that "includes";
- a term the text uses but leaves to an Act that is not among the sources, or a fact that lives there (the Employment Act, the Income Tax Act, the Supreme Court of Judicature Act, and so on): asked as an input, `NOTHING` when the input is not supplied;
- a bare "must" that was not done (the answer to "was the requirement met?" is `NOTHING`: judgment call QB; what follows non-compliance is O 3 r 2(4), in another part's range);
- a scope limit with no "only" ("apply to proceedings under the Employment Act") when the case is outside it;
- a power ("the Court may") when the case falls outside what the power names, or a grant limited by its own wording.

Where the result is a number (an amount, a number of days) it cannot carry `JUST FALSE`; the function that asks "does this rule apply?" is its yes/no twin (assumed, not ruled).
A caller must never turn `NOTHING` into `FALSE`.

**Every answer in the Rules carries a caveat that no function threads as an input**: O 3 r 2(1) says all requirements in the Rules are subject to the Court's discretion to order otherwise in the interests of justice, even if they are expressed using imperative words such as "must", "is to" or "shall" (line 296).
A reader of any `JUST TRUE` or `JUST FALSE` here should hold it in mind.
Where the text itself says "Unless the Court otherwise orders" the exception is stated by the rule, and the function takes the fact that the Court has ordered otherwise as an input.


## Seams (roc10)

Each seam is an answer that lives in another part's range or outside these Rules.
Layer 1 takes it as an input named for the section that answers it (PLAN.md section 5); the lead wires it at the merge.
Format: the asking provision; the answering provision; the input name; its type.
The layer-1 modules call two keel functions (`o 1 r 3(1) a court is the General Division` and `o 1 r 3(1) a person sitting in a court is a Judge`) and take one keel seam input (S-T1); a grep for `o 1 r` and `o 3 r` names in the layer-1 modules found no others.

### Seam inputs taken

- **S-T1.** Every stated period: the asking provisions are the 28 in the Time table below, `o 53 r 9(1)` (a period specified in a notice of registration, so it has no `Period` constant) and `o 51 r 4(3)` (next bullet); the answering provision is O 3 r 3 (roc1); the input is `o 3 r 3 the last day for doing the act`; type `DATE`.
- **A seam the plan lacks, in the S-T1 shape.** O 51 r 4(3) asks when "the time limited for filing and serving the notice of intention to contest or not contest" ends (line 6473).
  That time is fixed by another Order (Order 6, not in this part's range) and counted under O 3 r 3.
  `PLAN.md` section 5.3 lists, for O 51, only the citation of Order 9 Rule 17 (line 6477), so this one is not listed.
  This part reads it through the S-T1 input (`o 51 r 4(3) issue of the summons is barred`, roc10-order-51.l4:238); the lead should wire that call to the end of the time limited by Order 6, not to a period counted from anything in Order 51.
  O 52 r 4(2) is the same shape in reverse (a summons served "at least 4 clear days before the hearing") and is an ordinary S-T1 period.

### Which functions take the last day, and where the weekend exclusion is tested (checked, no change)

Thirty-four functions across 30 provisions take the S-T1 input `o 3 r 3 the last day for doing the act`.
By Order: O 46A r 1(2), r 1(3), r 2(b), r 4(2)(a), (b) and (d), r 5(2), r 6(2)(b); O 47 r 5(2), r 5(3); O 48 r 2(2), (3), (5) and (6), and r 6(5) (three functions); O 49 r 4(1), r 5(1), r 6(1); O 51 r 4(3) (two functions); O 52 r 2(3), r 4(2); O 53 r 2(3), r 9(1) (two functions), r 13(2), r 14(4), r 15(1), r 15A(1), r 16(4), r 18(1), r 19.
An act after that day answers JUST FALSE (reading (a) of Marginal readings), except `o 46A r 5(2)`, which answers NOTHING outside its nine months (fork 46A.2); the barred twins (`o 48 r 6(5)`, `o 51 r 4(3)`, `o 53 r 9(1)`) are the inverse, and their "may be" twins stay NOTHING.
The caller supplies the day already counted: this part never adds days.
Two consequences, both deliberate:
- The "partial" scenarios of an independent reading, which expect the last day of an O 3 r 3 count (for instance the 14th day after a service), ask this part to count; counting belongs to O 3 r 3 in another part, so there is no change here.
- The exclusion of non-court days that O 3 r 3(6) makes where the period is 6 days or less, and the next-day rule of r 3(7), belong to the part that encodes O 3 r 3 (roc1), and their tests are there, not here: this part cannot test them, because it never counts.
  A clear-day scenario in this part (for instance `o 53 r 13(2)`, 7 clear days) is tested with the day already counted.
Dates that are NOT S-T1 inputs: `o 53 r 12(2)` takes the day in question and the day fixed for the hearing with notice as two plain dates (the Court fixes the day; no period is counted).

### Cross-Order answers taken as inputs

- O 46A r 4(2) asks whether the application is one mentioned in Order 9 Rule 9(2) or Rule 9(7)(a) to (o): inputs `the party wishes to make a single application pending trial mentioned in Order 9, Rule 9(2)` and `the party wishes to make an application mentioned in Order 9, Rule 9(7)(a) to (o)`; type `BOOLEAN` (roc10-order-46a.l4:471).
- O 47 r 2(3) asks whether the proceedings are proceedings under Order 11 (production of documents): input `the proceedings are proceedings under Order 11`; `BOOLEAN` (roc10-order-47.l4:192 and roc10-order-47.l4:209); the barred twin `o 47 r 2(3) production of the document by the Comptroller is barred` takes the same inputs.
- O 55 r 4(2) asks whether the examination was taken in the manner provided by Order 9 Rule 24: input `the examination was taken in the manner provided by Order 9, Rule 24`; `BOOLEAN` (roc10-order-55.l4:284).
- O 56 r 5(3) asks whether a member was served out of Singapore with the permission of the Court given under Order 8: input `the member was, with the permission of the Court given under Order 8, served out of Singapore with the originating claim as a partner`; `BOOLEAN` (roc10-order-56.l4:519 and roc10-order-56.l4:535).
- O 56 r 7(2) and (3) ask about a notice of attachment under Order 22 Rule 6 and an order under Order 22 Rule 2: inputs `it is a notice of attachment under Order 22, Rule 6 relating to such debts` and `an order made under Order 22, Rule 2 requires a firm to appear before the Court`; `BOOLEAN` (roc10-order-56.l4:710 and roc10-order-56.l4:720 and roc10-order-56.l4:735).
- O 55 r 5(b) asks whether the document was sent "in accordance with a Civil Procedure Convention": taken as the `BOOLEAN` input `the document was sent to the Registrar by some other person in accordance with a Civil Procedure Convention`; the keel's function for a Civil Procedure Convention is not called (the grep above found only the two keel functions named).

### Overrides and "applies" statements (S-L1, S-L2): flat statements, no input

An override is encoded as a flat statement about the overridden provision, and the overridden part does not thread an input for it (PLAN.md S-L2).
The six overrides in the range are: O 46A r 3(1) over Order 9 Rule 8 (`o 46A r 3(1) Order 9, Rule 8 stops this Rule from applying`), O 47 r 6 over Order 26 Rule 3, O 50 r 2 over Order 56, O 52 r 4(1) over Order 6 Rules 6(5) and 7(7), O 53 r 3 over Order 26 Rule 3, and O 54 r 1(1) over Order 1 Rule 2.
The "applies with the necessary modifications" statements are flat statements that the cited provision applies and no more: `o 48 r 4(3)`, `o 48 r 6(4)`, `o 48 r 7`, `o 48 r 8`, `o 49 r 2(5)`, `o 52 r 4(3)`, `o 53 r 7(2)`, `o 53 r 14(5)`, `o 53 r 16(1)`, `o 53 r 17(5)`, `o 55 r 4(2)`, `o 56 r 8` and `o 56 r 9` (the function names are in the coverage table).

### Facts that live outside the sources (inputs, not seams)

The Acts named by these Orders are not among the sources, so their facts are inputs named for the Act and section: the Employment Act (O 46), the Income Tax Act, Part XXA and sections 65, 65A, 65B and 105D (O 47), the Supreme Court of Judicature Act 1969, Fourth Schedule paragraph 4(1) (O 46A r 1(5)), the International Arbitration Act and the Model Law (O 48), the Land Titles (Strata) Act and the Building Maintenance and Strata Management Act (O 49), the Limited Liability Partnerships Act (O 50), the Moneylenders Act section 2 (O 51), the Mutual Assistance in Criminal Matters Act and its Third Schedule (O 53), the Oaths and Declarations Act with Forms 82 and 83 (O 54) and the Partnership Act section 23 (O 56 r 10).
A caller who does not supply one of them gets `NOTHING` where the function takes a `MAYBE` input, and must supply a `BOOLEAN` input otherwise.

### Time (every period in the range)

Each period is a `Period` constant named for its provision; the function that tests an act against it takes the seam input `o 3 r 3 the last day for doing the act` (S-T1, answered in roc1) and answers `JUST TRUE` on or before it, `JUST FALSE` after it.
Counting follows O 3 r 3 (lines 323 to 329): a period "after" or "from" a date begins immediately after it (r 3(3)); where an act is to be done within, or not less than, a specified period before a date, the period ends immediately before that date (r 3(4)); "clear days" means at least that number of days intervene (r 3(5)); a period of 6 days or less leaves out non-court days (r 3(6)); a period that expires on a non-court day runs to the next court day (r 3(7)); a month is a calendar month (r 3(2)).
The counting is the lead's: the seam input arrives already counted, so no function here adds days itself.

## Dependencies

Nothing was copied from `inputs/DEPENDENCIES/`, and nothing needed: the Interpretation Act module staged there governs periods an Act sets, and O 3 r 3(1) (line 324) says that "The Interpretation Act does not apply to the calculation of time in these Rules".
Every period in this range is a period of these Rules (counted under O 3 r 3, lines 323 to 329) or is set by another Act that is not among the sources (taken as an input).
`encoding.json` is written by the lead; the key `"dependencies"` for this part would read `[]`.


## Unstable dialect

A second encoding in the unstable dialect of some of these provisions was not written: I looked at time, versions and workarounds in lines 6102 to 6795 and found none that earns one.

- **Time.** Every period in the range is a length counted from a date the asker supplies (28 `Period` constants, listed in the Time table under "Seams (roc10)"), and the keel's O 3 r 3 supplies the last day as a seam input; no rule in the range is in force on different dates.
  The text carries 18 amendment notes ("wef"): 12 read `S 210/2025 wef 28/03/2025` and 6 read `S 504/2024 wef 01/07/2024` (`grep -o -E 'wef [0-9/]+'` over the range, 2026-11-05).
  All of those dates are earlier than the text's "current version as at 5 November 2026", and the text gives only the current version, so the amended wording is the only wording to encode and no earlier version is available to pair with it.
- **Versions.** No provision of the range says it takes effect on a later date or applies only to proceedings begun before or after a date; the only "begun" and "placed on" dates (O 46A r 1(7)) are dates the asker supplies.
- **Workarounds.** The one place the main dialect made me work around a gap is `#ASSERT` on a `DEONTIC` value (the regulative rules in `roc10-duties.l4`): I probed the unstable build `unstable-20261104-68498bc` with a one-rule file and it also refuses ("Trying to check equality on types that do not support it", the same message as the main build), so unstable would not express it better and the workaround stands (run `#TRACE`, record the outcome by hand, assert the decision-function twin).
  The probe was a throwaway file in `work/probe`; the unstable binary was not used for anything in the main row.


## Findings

### What the text turns out to say

1. **Six Orders fix no counted period, and ten periods in the range are counted in clear days.**
   Orders 46, 50, 51, 54, 55 and 56 have no `Period` constant (the Time table under "Seams (roc10)" lists all 28 periods of the range and none is from them).
   Seven of the ten clear-day periods are the 7 clear days before a hearing in O 53 (rr. 13(2), 14(4), 15(1), 15A(1), 16(4), 18(1) and 19); the other three are O 52 r 2(3) (2 clear days), O 52 r 4(2) (4 clear days) and O 53 r 2(3) (2 clear days).
2. **"Unless the Court otherwise orders" and its variants are frequent.**
   In lines 6102 to 6795 the phrase "otherwise orders" occurs 11 times and "otherwise directs" 7 times, with "otherwise provided" 3 times and "subject to any directions" once (`grep -o -i -E` over the range, 2026-11-05).
   Each is encoded as a fact the asker supplies (for example `the Court has otherwise ordered`), and it lifts a duty (JUST FALSE) or a bar (NOTHING) according to the shape of the sentence (judgment call QA and QD).
3. **Some duties have an actor and no time, so a trace cannot breach them by delay.**
   O 53 r 21(3) (the authorised officer or process server makes an affidavit) and (4) (the Registrar issues the certificate or the statement), O 54 r 2(3) (the officer administers the oath) and O 55 r 5 (the examiner sends the deposition; the Registrar gives and sends the certificate) state no time; in `roc10-duties.l4` they have no `WITHIN`, and a trace that stops after the first step leaves the next duty residual.
4. **O 56 r 2(3) changes the Court's power by omission.**
   Paragraph (1)(b) (a stay) is "omitted" for an action against partners in the name of a firm (lines 6732 to 6735), so the Court's order in that action is the Form 84 order alone (fork 56.1).
5. **O 50 r 2 switches off the whole of Order 56 for a limited liability partnership registered under the Act.**
   The sentence is "Order 56 does not apply to a limited liability partnership registered under the Act" (line 6429); `o 50 r 2 Order 56 applies to the partnership` answers JUST FALSE for such a partnership.
6. **O 49 r 4(2) lists eleven particulars twice.**
   Paragraph (2)(a) (an application under section 84A(1) or 84FA(2)) and (2)(b) (an application under section 84D(2) or 84E(3)) each list the eleven particulars (i) to (xi) in different wording (lines 6346 to 6391); they are encoded as two separate eleven-field records so that a caller sees which wording applies.

### What the main binary does not accept

Sorted by how the failure shows up (CLAUDE.md rule 7: the quiet ones first).

- **Silent: `NOT (a) OR b` is `NOT ((a) OR b)`.**
  Measured on 1.6.109 and 1.6.108 with `work/probe/pnot.l4`: with `a` and `b` both `TRUE`, `NOT (a) OR b` evaluates to `FALSE` and `(NOT (a)) OR b` to `TRUE`; both runs exit 0 with every assertion satisfied.
  A grep for `NOT (...) AND` and `NOT (...) OR` across the layer-1 modules after the Orders were written found none left unparenthesised; every NOT in this part is written `(NOT (x)) OR y`.
- **Silent: `l4 check` does not run assertions.**
  It prints no assertion result, so a module with a wrong expected value passes `l4 check`; the counts in the Check section come from `l4 run` through `check.sh`, which also compares both builds.
- **Loud: `` `WAIT UNTIL` n `` is not defined on the main builds.**
  `l4-ide`'s regulative reference writes ``(`WAIT UNTIL` 31)`` for a clock advance; on 1.6.109 and 1.6.108 it gives `I could not find a definition for the identifier `WAIT UNTIL``, and `WAITUNTIL 15` (one word) is accepted; both builds then print the same BREACH.
- **Loud: a regulative rule must sit inside `GIVETH A DEONTIC <party> <action>` / `DECIDE ... IS`.**
  At the top level it is a parse error (`unexpected PARTY`).
- **Loud: `#ASSERT` on a `DEONTIC` value fails** with "assertion could not be evaluated: Trying to check equality on types that do not support it" (probe `work/probe/pdeon.l4`, 1.6.109 and the unstable build `unstable-20261104-68498bc`).
  A deontic rule's outcome cannot be asserted: `roc10-duties.l4` runs each duty with `#TRACE`, writes the outcome beside the trace, and `roc10-tests-duties.l4` asserts the decision-function twin of the same facts.
  The trace outcomes were read from `l4 run` on 1.6.109 and 1.6.108 and were identical (a `diff` of the two outputs printed nothing); `check.sh` does not compare traces, so that diff is the only evidence.


## What the text does not answer (roc10)

Questions a user might ask that this part cannot answer, because the text does not, or because the answer is in an instrument that is not among the sources.
Each is either a `NOTHING` the functions return on purpose (the comment kind is named) or a question the records hold the facts for and no function asks.
The counts come from the `NOTHING-kind (...)` comments in the layer-1 modules (`grep -h -o 'NOTHING-kind ([^)]*)' roc10-order-*.l4 | sort | uniq -c`, run on 2026-11-05 after the fix round): 211 `silent`, 212 `limited by its own wording`, 9 `points outside the text`, 6 `list not closed`, 6 `includes`.

### Answers that live outside the sources (9 sites, comment kind `points outside the text`)

- O 46A r 1(5), twice: the circumstances in paragraph 4(1) of the Fourth Schedule to the Supreme Court of Judicature Act 1969.
- O 48 r 1, three definitions ("arbitral tribunal", "award", "foreign award") that take their meaning from Parts II and III of the International Arbitration Act.
- O 48 r 2(7): the date of receipt of an award, which "is to be determined in accordance with Article 3 of the Model Law".
- O 49 r 2(1): the time "limited by section 84A(2B), 84D(2B), 84E(3B) or 84FA(2B), whichever is applicable" of the Land Titles (Strata) Act.
- O 51 r 1(2): "moneylender" has the meaning in section 2 of the Moneylenders Act.
- O 56 r 5(3): "except as provided by this paragraph and paragraph (1)" hands the answer to those provisions of the same rule (the comment kind is `points outside the text`, the answer lies in the rule itself).
The facts of the Acts are inputs, named for the Act and section; see "Facts that live outside the sources" under Seams.

### Lists the text does not close (6 sites, `list not closed`) and "includes" (6 sites, `includes`)

- Lists without the word "only": O 47 r 2(4) (the documents), O 48 r 2(1) (the four kinds of application to a Judge, two sites), O 48 r 3(1) (the six kinds of application or request to the Court, two sites), O 56 r 5(2) (four kinds of person).
  A thing outside the list answers `NOTHING`: the text neither includes nor excludes it.
- "Includes" and "including": O 46A r 1(4), O 49 r 4(3), O 50 r 3(2) (three orders "in particular"), O 52 r 1(2) ("mortgage"), O 53 r 12(1), O 56 r 6(2).
  The named things are in, and the list is open.

### Counts the records hold and no function asks

The text lists the items, the record holds one field for each, and no function returns how many there are.
- O 46 r 3(1): 7 particulars of the certificate (`o 46 r 3(1) what the certificate states`).
- O 47 r 5(2): 3 persons to be served (the Comptroller; the person from whom the Comptroller obtains the information; the person in relation to whom information is sought).
- O 49 r 4(2)(a) and (b): 11 particulars each, in different wording (`o 49 r 4(2)(a) what the affidavit sets out`).
- O 51 r 3: 11 particulars of the statement of claim (`o 51 r 3 what the statement of claim states`).

### Points where the text is silent about a consequence or a person

- What follows when a bare "must" is not met: the QB sites in the Judgment calls register answer `NOTHING`, because O 3 r 2(1) and (4) leave it to the Court.
- O 46A r 5(2): "as far as possible" makes the nine months a target, so a trial fixed later answers `NOTHING` (fork 46A.2).

The baseline it replaced: none.
This is a new part; before it there was no `roc10` module in the deposit, and the only modules were the two keel files (`roc-core.l4`, `roc-definitions.l4`), which print zeros in every column.
The total went from 0 satisfied (the keel alone) to 1932 satisfied in 30 modules at the first report, and to 1963 satisfied in 30 modules after the fix round (a `diff` of the `#ASSERT` lines against the copy taken before the fix round shows 40 lines removed or replaced and 71 written, net +31), with 0 errors, 0 failed and 0 refused, identical on both builds.

Other checks run at the same time:

- `python3 -I srcrefs.py check --refs <text> roc10-*.l4`: `srcrefs check: 637 src: lines, 276 runs, 421 locators, 0 problems`; the same with `--strict`: 0 problems.
- `l4 check` on every one of the 28 `roc10-*.l4` modules with the 1.6.109 build, filtered for `is at the end of the file` and `follows code on its line`: no output.
- Coverage: 581 units, 581 rows, 0 reading MISSING, 0 non-blank lines of 6102 to 6795 outside a row (generated by `work/tools/mknotes.py`, which is not shipped).
- The `#TRACE` outcomes in `roc10-duties.l4` (which `check.sh` does not compare) were read from `l4 run` on both builds and were identical.
